Let me run a tighter query — aggregated at the pattern level with coverage per channel — to give you clean, usable logic.# Derivation Logic + Coverage for the 3 Missing Event Flags

Based on 3 days of UDI (~836M rows total), here's what each concept looks like in actual production data.

📞 1. event_click_to_call — Phone call initiations
What exists: Very few hits literally use "click to call" / "tap to call" / "call us" phrasing. Click-to-call is not a well-tagged pattern in UDI — most phone-tap behavior is embedded inside support flows and not labeled with a dedicated action.

Coverage (last 3 days)
Channel	Matching rows	Total rows	% Fill
App	18,249	702.8M	0.0026%
Web	2,717	133.4M	0.0020%
Suggested derivation (best approximation)
```
CASE
WHEN attribute_event_action ILIKE '%click to call%'
OR attribute_event_action ILIKE '%click-to-call%'
OR attribute_event_action ILIKE '%tap to call%'
OR attribute_event_action ILIKE '%call us%'
THEN 1 ELSE 0
END AS event_click_to_call
```

⚠️ Realistic expectation: This flag will fire on ~1 in 50,000 rows. If "click to call" matters to your consumers, flag this as a tagging gap — don't present the derived column as reliable.

💬 2. event_chat_engage — Chat interactions
What exists: Chat is very well tagged — both via attribute_event_category (whole category) and attribute_event_action (specific chat moments). Key categories: Chat, JourneyBot, Live Agent. Top actions include Chat Topics Viewed, Chatbot Screen Load, Chat Click, Chat Entry Click, Live Agent Chat Initiation.

Coverage (last 3 days)
Channel	Matching rows	Total rows	% Fill
App	15,089,657	702.8M	2.15%
Web	1,128,208	133.4M	0.85%
Suggested derivation — pick based on intent
```
-- Broad: any chat touchpoint (screen view, button click, bot interaction)
CASE
WHEN attribute_event_category ILIKE '%chat%'
OR attribute_event_category ILIKE '%journeybot%'
OR attribute_event_action ILIKE '%chat%'
THEN 1 ELSE 0
END AS event_chat_engage
```

Narrower alternative — true engagement only (initiated or messaged, not just screen view):
```
CASE
WHEN attribute_event_action IN (
'Chat Click', 'Chat Entry Click', 'Chat Message Engaged',
'Live Agent Chat Initiation', 'Chat Session Ended', 'Chat Ended By User'
)
OR attribute_event_action ILIKE '%chat with customer care%click%'
THEN 1 ELSE 0
END AS event_chat_engage_strict
```

The broad version matches ~2% of app hits; the strict version will land around 0.3–0.5%. Choose based on whether your downstream consumers want "touched chat" or "actually engaged chat."

🏪 3. event_store_search — Store locator searches
What exists: Also lightly tagged. Store locator usage mostly fires as page views (page_url_path containing /stores/ or /store-locator), not as a dedicated event action.

Coverage (last 3 days — using action-string match only)
Channel	Matching rows	Total rows	% Fill
App	252	702.8M	~0%
Web	6,114	133.4M	0.0046%
Suggested derivation — combine action + URL
```
CASE
WHEN attribute_event_action ILIKE '%store locator%'
OR attribute_event_action ILIKE '%find a store%'
OR attribute_event_action ILIKE '%find store%'
OR attribute_event_action ILIKE '%store search%'
OR page_url_path ILIKE '%/stores/%'
OR page_url_path ILIKE '%/store-locator%'
OR page_name ILIKE '%store locator%'
THEN 1 ELSE 0
END AS event_store_search
```

Adding the URL/page_name signals will boost web coverage significantly (likely to 0.1–0.3% of web rows). App store-locator is nearly nonexistent — likely because the T-Life app has very different store discovery UX.

📝 Recommendations for your MIP projection
Flag	Verdict	Recommendation
event_click_to_call	🔴 Not reliably tagged (~0.002%)	Include the derivation but flag as low-fidelity in your MIP contract notes. Consider filing a tagging-gap ticket.
event_chat_engage	🟢 Well tagged (~1–2%)	Include confidently. Pick broad or strict based on consumer need.
event_store_search	🟡 Partial — mostly URL-based	Use the combined action+URL rule, not action alone. Document it as approximate.




Define peer set and topline metrics.

### Impact on topline

**Plain version:** how much of the topline's movement this one value is responsible for. It answers "so what" rather than "how much did this change."

**Formula, counts:**

```
impact % = (slice current − slice baseline) ÷ topline baseline
```

The denominator is the topline, not the slice's own base. That's the whole trick.

**Example:**

```
Paid Search  (493,775 − 446,463) ÷ 1,720,000  =  +2.75%
SMS          ( 31,094 − 202,108) ÷ 1,720,000  =  −9.94%
```

Compare against what each did on its own base: Paid Search rose 10.6%, SMS fell 84.6%. Sorted by own change, SMS looks like the story of the week and Paid Search looks minor. Sorted by impact, SMS moved the topline by ten points and Paid Search by three. That ordering is the one you want in the weekly read.

## Versus peer set — worked through step by step

**The question it answers:** did this channel move differently from all the other channels?

### The setup

Channel breakout, Total UPV, week 7 compared to week 6.

Week 6

Week 7

**All channels together (topline)**

1,720,000

1,545,000

**Paid Search**

446,463

493,775

The topline fell. Paid Search rose. That gap is what we're measuring.

### Step 1 — What did Paid Search do?

```
493,775 − 446,463 = +47,312
+47,312 ÷ 446,463 = +10.6%
```

**Paid Search went up 10.6%.**

### Step 2 — What did every other channel do?

Take Paid Search out of the topline. What's left is the peer set: organic, direct, email, SMS, display, and the rest, all added together.

```
Week 6 peers:  1,720,000 − 446,463 = 1,273,537
Week 7 peers:  1,545,000 − 493,775 = 1,051,225
```

Now the same change calculation on that combined group:

```
1,051,225 − 1,273,537 = −222,312
−222,312 ÷ 1,273,537 = −17.5%
```

**Every other channel, combined, went down 17.5%.**

### Step 3 — The points gap

```
Paid Search:   +10.6%
Peer set:      −17.5%
Gap:            10.6 − (−17.5) = +28.1 points
```

**Paid Search finished 28.1 points ahead of the rest of the channel mix.**

### Step 4 — Turn that into volume

Points are hard to act on. So: what would Paid Search have looked like if it had behaved like everything else?

```
Apply the peer rate to Paid Search's starting point:
446,463 × (1 − 0.175) = 368,527
```

That's the counterfactual. Now compare it to reality:

```
Actual:            493,775
Would have been:   368,527
Excess:           +125,248
```

**125,248 UPV exists only because Paid Search moved against the tide.**

### How to say it out loud

> Every other channel dropped about 17% this week. Paid Search went up 10%. If it had dropped with everything else it would have come in around 369,000. It came in at 494,000. So roughly 125,000 visits are down to something happening specifically in Paid Search.

### The same four steps on SMS, for contrast

```
1. SMS:        31,094 vs 202,108     →  −84.6%
2. Peer set:   1,513,906 vs 1,517,892 →   −0.3%
3. Gap:        −84.6 − (−0.3)         =  −84.4 points
4. Would have been: 202,108 × 0.997   =  201,577
   Excess:     31,094 − 201,577       =  −170,483
```

The rest of the channels were flat. SMS fell off a cliff. Nothing systemic is going on, so the answer is entirely inside SMS.

### Two things the engineers need to know

**The peer set always uses the four-week trend**, whatever comparison the user has picked on screen. The example above uses prior week because it's easier to follow.

**Step 2 cannot be done by subtraction on real data.** It worked here because the sample file was built so the channels add up. Real UPV doesn't: one person visits through search and direct in the same week and counts in both, so the channels sum to more than the topline. Subtracting Paid Search from 1,720,000 would give the wrong peer figure.

On EDL, step 2 means going back to the visit-level table and counting distinct visitors across all channels except Paid Search. Then repeating that for every channel, every breakout, every metric, every week. Page views is the only metric where the shortcut is valid.


In Databricks, the underlying execution engine is Spark regardless of whether we use SQL or PySpark, so switching between them does not inherently improve performance. We keep them separate mainly for maintainability: SQL is cleaner for data transformations, joins, aggregations, and Delta operations, while Python is better suited for orchestration, parameters, validation, logging, and workflow control.


I wanted to clarify one point on the peer-set definition for unique visitor metrics.
For example, say Visitor A comes through both Paid Search and Direct during the same week. When calculating the peer set for Paid Search, should Visitor A:
1. Be excluded from the peer set because they had any Paid Search activity during the week, even though they also used Direct?
or
2. Be assigned to one attributed weekly channel first, and only excluded if Paid Search is that attributed channel?
For example, if we have:
Paid Search visitors: A, B, C  
Direct visitors: A, D, E
For the Paid Search peer set, should we count D and E only, because A touched Paid Search and therefore must be excluded? Or if A's attributed weekly channel is Direct, should A, D, E all be part of the Paid Search peer set?
I want to confirm because the first definition requires us to retain all channel memberships per visitor/week, whereas the second lets us use the single attributed weekly channel we already have.


SELECT
    sourceTable,

    COUNT(*) AS rows,

    COUNT_IF(
        sessionEntryPageUrlFull IS NOT NULL
        AND TRIM(sessionEntryPageUrlFull) <> ''
    ) AS rowsWithEntryUrl,

    COUNT_IF(utmSource <> '(not set)') AS rowsWithUtmSource,
    COUNT_IF(utmMedium <> '(not set)') AS rowsWithUtmMedium,
    COUNT_IF(utmCampaign <> '(not set)') AS rowsWithUtmCampaign,

    COUNT_IF(channelName IS NOT NULL) AS rowsWithChannel,
    COUNT_IF(externalCampaignCode IS NOT NULL) AS rowsWithExternalCampaign

FROM prdrzranalytics.lab42.sdi_tbl_mip_silver_detailsPerHit_daily
WHERE eventDate BETWEEN DATE '2026-09-20' AND DATE '2026-09-29'
GROUP BY sourceTable
ORDER BY sourceTable;

Let me search both the Silver dictionary and Bronze tables for UTM-related fields.# UTM Tracking — What's Available and What's Not

🎯 Summary
Channel	Direct UTM columns in Silver?	What you can use instead
Web (t_web_interactions)	❌ No dedicated utm_* columns	Parse from page_url_full OR use external_campaign_code (Adobe's parsed campaign)
App (t_app_interactions)	❌ No dedicated utm_* columns	Use external_campaign_code (Adobe campaign code from deep-link / referrer)
Silver does not expose discrete `utm_source`, `utm_medium`, `utm_campaign`, `utm_term`, or `utm_content` columns. T-Mobile's digital interactions pipeline rolls UTM data up into Adobe's s.campaign variable, which lands in Silver as external_campaign_code.

Let me verify coverage so you know what to expect.## 📊 UTM Signal Coverage — Last 3 Days

Only ~4% of web hits actually carry raw UTM parameters in page_url_full. The rest are either organic/direct traffic or landing pages where UTM was stripped after first-touch.

🔧 How to Extract Individual UTM Parameters
Web — parse from page_url_full
Databricks SQL (regex-based):

SELECT
  page_url_full,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(page_url_full, '[?&]utm_source=([^&#]+)',   1) AS utm_source,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(page_url_full, '[?&]utm_medium=([^&#]+)',   1) AS utm_medium,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(page_url_full, '[?&]utm_campaign=([^&#]+)', 1) AS utm_campaign,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(page_url_full, '[?&]utm_term=([^&#]+)',     1) AS utm_term,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(page_url_full, '[?&]utm_content=([^&#]+)',  1) AS utm_content,
  external_campaign_code  -- Adobe's already-parsed campaign ID
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions
WHERE source_table = 't_web_interactions'
  AND event_date = DATE '2026-10-02'
  AND page_url_full ILIKE '%utm_%'
LIMIT 100
App — use external_campaign_code
Apps rarely surface raw UTM strings — campaigns come in via deep-link attribution and land already-parsed in external_campaign_code:

SELECT
  external_campaign_code,
  COUNT(*) AS hits
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions
WHERE source_table = 't_app_interactions'
  AND event_date = DATE '2026-10-02'
  AND external_campaign_code IS NOT NULL
GROUP BY 1
ORDER BY hits DESC
LIMIT 50
If T-Mobile uses a convention like utm_source|utm_medium|utm_campaign concatenated in that field, you can split it:

SPLIT(external_campaign_code, '|')[0] AS campaign_source,
SPLIT(external_campaign_code, '|')[1] AS campaign_medium,
SPLIT(external_campaign_code, '|')[2] AS campaign_name
🪤 Important caveats
UTM only fires on entry hits. The ?utm_* query params exist only on the landing page URL — once a user navigates deeper, UTM is gone. For session-level attribution, you need to grab UTM from the first hit of each session (join to SESSION_SUMMARY_FACT on session_id and pull entry-page UTM, then propagate forward).
App UTM is near-zero because of OS-level deep-link rewriting. The ~0.02% fill isn't a data bug — iOS/Android campaign IDs get consumed by install attribution SDKs (Adjust/Branch/AppsFlyer) and don't persist as UTM-style strings in the hit stream. For app campaign attribution, you likely need an attribution-partner table outside UDI.
If you need durable, cross-channel UTM, consider filing a Jira under DIRT-691 to add discrete utm_source / utm_medium / utm_campaign columns to Silver, parsed once at ingestion from page_url_full.

🔍 What this tells you about T-Mobile's UTM taxonomy
Dominant sources: google (paid search), email (CRM/Tuesdays), instagram / facebook (paid social), vce (internal/vendor channel), bing
Mediums use snake_case: cpc, paidsocial, t_mobile_tuesdays, multiline, email
Campaign names are structured strings with pipe-free, underscore-delimited taxonomy encoding product line, audience, locale, placement, and week — e.g., tt_app_wk38_tue_v2_ios_t-life_cm_generalterms_generalterms_text_09232026 encodes week 38, iOS, T-Life, Tuesdays rewards.
Lots of T-Mobile Tuesdays email traffic — rows 2–3 alone drive ~56K hits.
💡 Practical takeaways
For web campaign reporting, both external_campaign_code (5.3% fill) and parsed UTM parameters (3.6% fill) work — external_campaign_code is slightly broader because it survives beyond the entry hit.
For session-level attribution, join to SESSION_SUMMARY_FACT and take the UTM from the entry hit only (use hit_number_in_session = 1 in SESSION_EVENT_FACT).
For app, UTM parsing is a dead end. If you need app campaign attribution, you'll need to pull from the attribution partner (Adjust/Branch) outside UDI, or lean on external_campaign_code for the tiny fraction that has it.

UTM Availability — Honest Summary
🎯 Direct answer: No, not really for App
Channel	UTM Available?	Via What
Web	✅ Yes, partial	Parse from page_url_full or use external_campaign_code
App	❌ Effectively no	external_campaign_code exists but fills only ~0.02% — not usable for reporting
So in practice, UTM-style campaign attribution works for Web only. The App has a column (external_campaign_code) that could carry it, but near-zero fill means it's not a real signal.

📍 Tables and Columns — exactly what to use
Primary table (both channels)
`prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions`
(Snowflake equivalent: BDM_DBI_DB.SILVER_DIGITAL_INTERACTIONS.UNIFIED_DIGITAL_INTERACTIONS)

Columns involved
Column	Scope	Fill Rate	Purpose
page_url_full	Web only	~100% on web, 0% on app	Full landing URL — contains raw ?utm_source=…&utm_medium=…&utm_campaign=… query params on ~3.6% of web hits
external_campaign_code	Both channels (nominally)	Web ~5–6%, App ~0.02%	Adobe's already-parsed campaign ID (s.campaign → v0). Pre-propagated across the session on web; nearly empty on app
source_table	Both	100%	Filter: 't_web_interactions' vs 't_app_interactions'
How to pick between the two web approaches
Need	Use
Discrete utm_source / utm_medium / utm_campaign values	Parse page_url_full with REGEXP_EXTRACT
Broader coverage (5–6% vs 3.6%) and session-wide propagation	Use external_campaign_code
Both	Pull page_url_full for the entry hit of each session, parse once, then propagate forward yourself
🧭 Entry-hit attribution pattern (recommended for sessions)
For proper session-level campaign attribution, you want the UTM from the entry hit only, then propagate across the session. That uses two more tables:

Supporting tables for session-level attribution
Table	Role
prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_summary_fact	Session grain — one row per session, has session_id, canonical_user_id
prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_event_fact	Event grain — has session_id, hit_number_in_session, row_identity_hash to join back to UDI
Join pattern
WITH entry_hit AS (
  SELECT
    e.session_id,
    u.page_url_full,
    u.external_campaign_code,
    REGEXP_EXTRACT(u.page_url_full, '[?&]utm_source=([^&#]+)',   1) AS utm_source,
    REGEXP_EXTRACT(u.page_url_full, '[?&]utm_medium=([^&#]+)',   1) AS utm_medium,
    REGEXP_EXTRACT(u.page_url_full, '[?&]utm_campaign=([^&#]+)', 1) AS utm_campaign
  FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_event_fact e
  JOIN prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions u
    ON  e.row_identity_hash = u.row_identity_hash
    AND e.event_date        = u.event_date
    AND e.source_table      = u.source_table
  WHERE e.event_date = DATE '2026-09-24'
    AND e.hit_number_in_session = 1       -- entry hit only
    AND e.source_table = 't_web_interactions'
)
SELECT
  s.session_id,
  s.canonical_user_id,
  s.session_start_date,
  s.session_had_purchase,
  eh.utm_source, eh.utm_medium, eh.utm_campaign,
  eh.external_campaign_code
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_summary_fact s
LEFT JOIN entry_hit eh USING (session_id)
WHERE s.session_status IN ('OPEN','CLOSED')
  AND s.session_start_date = DATE '2026-09-24'
  AND s.source_table = 't_web_interactions'
🚧 App attribution — what to do instead
Since external_campaign_code is effectively empty for app, you have two realistic options:

Deep-link attribution partner data — T-Mobile uses an attribution SDK (Adjust / Branch / AppsFlyer) for app install and re-engagement campaigns. That data lands in a separate pipeline, not in UDI. Check with your Marketing Analytics team for the campaign attribution table in BDM_MKTGANALYTICS_DB.
File a tagging-gap ticket under DIRT-691 to request that app deep-link UTM parameters be captured into UDI (either as discrete fields or inside external_campaign_code).
📝 Bottom line
One source table: prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions
Two columns matter: page_url_full (web only, parse UTM from it) and external_campaign_code (both channels but only usable on web)
For session-level attribution: add session_event_fact + session_summary_fact and filter to hit_number_in_session = 1
For app: this isn't the right dataset — you need Adjust/Branch/AppsFlyer data

Hit-level page_url_full UTM parsing	Hit	3.6% of hits	Entry hits only
Hit-level external_campaign_code	Hit	5–6% of hits	Session-propagated

📊 Sep 24 Session-Level UTM Coverage
Web (t_web_interactions)

💡 What this means
Web: ~10% of web sessions carry UTM tagging on their entry URL — that's nearly 3× richer than the 3.6% hit-level view, because entry_page_url_full captures the first landing URL for the entire session (not just hits that happen to still have ?utm_* in them).
`entry_page_url_full` fills on 99.99% of web sessions — essentially every web session has an entry URL recorded.
App remains blank — entry_page_url_full is NULL on 100% of app sessions. Apps don't have "URLs" in the traditional sense, so this field is web-only.
⚠️ Access control reminder
entry_page_url_full is one of the access-controlled URL columns on SESSION_SUMMARY_FACT. Without the access group, it returns REDACTED. The ~100% fill above means the role I queried under has access — confirm your role does too, or your UTM parse will return nulls across the board.

The access-controlled column list on SESSION_SUMMARY_FACT:
- entry_page_url_full
- entry_page_url_path
- entry_page_referrer
- exit_page_url_full
- exit_page_url_path

🔧 Recommended pattern — session-level UTM from entry_page_url_full
SELECT
  session_id,
  canonical_user_id,
  session_start_date,
  session_had_purchase,
  entry_page_url_full,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_source=([^&#]+)',   1) AS utm_source,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_medium=([^&#]+)',   1) AS utm_medium,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_campaign=([^&#]+)', 1) AS utm_campaign,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_term=([^&#]+)',     1) AS utm_term,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_content=([^&#]+)',  1) AS utm_content,
  entry_page_referrer
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_summary_fact
WHERE session_status IN ('OPEN','CLOSED')
  AND source_table = 't_web_interactions'
  AND session_start_date = DATE '2026-09-24'
  AND entry_page_url_full ILIKE '%utm_%'
This is cleaner, higher-coverage, and session-grain — much better than parsing hit URLs and joining back through SEF.

📝 Updated recommendation for your Bronze/attribution pipeline
Use case	Best source
Web session-level campaign attribution	session_summary_fact.entry_page_url_full → regex extract
Web hit-level campaign tagging	external_campaign_code (5–6% fill, propagated across session)
App campaign attribution	❌ Not available in UDI/SEF/SSF — need Adjust/Branch/AppsFlyer data
Also worth noting: entry_page_referrer on SESSION_SUMMARY_FACT is also access-controlled and useful — it tells you where the user came from before landing (direct, google.com, facebook.com, etc.), which fills attribution gaps when UTM is missing.

I'll check coverage for all the channel-related columns across web and app on Sep 24.# Channel Column Coverage — Sep 24, 2026

Four columns in Silver carry channel/section info. Here's how each fills by source:

📊 Hit-Level Coverage (UDI)
Channel	Column	Web Fill %	App Fill %
channel	Site section (Shop, Account, Billing...)	99.9%	0.4%
attribute_channel	App-side channel grouping	0%	61.7%
channel_name	Numeric channel name	99.3%	7.5%
site_sub_section	Sub-section under channel	97.9%	0%
Total rows: Web 42.5M, App 234.3M.

🧭 Interpretation — which column to use when
Use channel for Web (99.9% fill)
This is the primary site section bucket on web. Values are English (and some Spanish) like Shop, Account, Billing, Support, Benefits, Home, Discover, Tuesdays, T-Mobile Money. Essentially every web hit has it.

Use attribute_channel for App (61.7% fill)
This is the app-side analog with values like Shop, Me, Home, Benefits, Connect, HSI, Network Pass, Onboarding, Tuesdays, Experience. The 38% gap is real — many app events (notifications, background syncs, splash screens, auth events) fire outside a named tab/channel.

channel_name is a numeric/id variant
Fills on 99% of web, 7% of app. Rarely the right column for human-readable reporting — prefer channel or attribute_channel.

site_sub_section is web-only (97.9% web, 0% app)
Gives you the next level down from channel — e.g., channel = 'Shop', site_sub_section = 'Phones'. Great for drill-down on web but offers nothing for app.

🔧 Unified channel column (recommended pattern)
If you need a single cross-channel "channel" field for a Bronze/MIP projection:

COALESCE(
  CASE WHEN source_table = 't_web_interactions' THEN channel           END,
  CASE WHEN source_table = 't_app_interactions' THEN attribute_channel END
) AS unified_channel
Expected combined fill with this pattern on Sep 24:
- Web contribution: ~99.9% of 42.5M = ~42.5M rows
- App contribution: ~61.7% of 234.3M = ~144.6M rows
- Combined: ~67.5% of all 276.8M hits

🪤 Caveats
Values are not aligned across channels. Web channel = 'Account' vs app attribute_channel = 'Me' both mean "my account" but are different strings. If you're reporting cross-channel, build a mapping table or normalize both to a common vocabulary (e.g., 'MyAccount').
~100 distinct values in `channel` including Spanish variants (Mi Cuenta ↔ My Account, Compra ↔ Shop). Normalize before grouping.
App's 38% null `attribute_channel` will skew any app denominator — decide whether unknowns go into '(unassigned)' or get excluded.

02:21 AM
Eddy
App Channels — You Do Have Them, Just in a Different Column
Short answer: Yes, app has channels — but in `attribute_channel`, not `channel`.

📊 The two columns explained
Column	Where it fires	Why
channel	Web only (99.9% fill)	Web uses Adobe's s.channel variable, populated by the TMS tagging on every page
attribute_channel	App only (61.7% fill)	App uses its own event-attribute payload (attribute.channel) from the app analytics SDK
They are the same business concept — the top-level nav tab/section the user is in — but they come from two different instrumentation stacks (Adobe web TMS vs. app SDK), so they land in different Silver columns. Nobody unified them into a single channel column during Silver ingestion.

🔍 What app channels look like
Top attribute_channel values on app: Shop, Me, Home, Benefits, Connect, HSI, Network Pass, Onboarding, Tuesdays, Experience.

These map to the actual T-Life app tabs. So yes — real channel info, just under a different name.

⚠️ Why the 38% gap on app
The ~38% of app hits with no attribute_channel are typically:
- Splash / launch screens — fire before a tab is selected
- Auth / login events — happen outside tab navigation
- Push notification opens — enter the app via deep link, not a tab
- Background sync / telemetry events — not tied to user navigation
- Interstitial / modal events — overlay on top of a tab without re-tagging

So the 38% isn't a tagging bug — it's genuinely "user wasn't in a channel at that moment."

🔧 How to use channels cross-channel
Build the unified column once in your Bronze layer:

CASE
  WHEN source_table = 't_web_interactions' THEN channel
  WHEN source_table = 't_app_interactions' THEN attribute_channel
END AS unified_channel
Then downstream reporting can use unified_channel without caring which stack produced it.

🪤 One caveat — value vocabularies differ
Even where both channels tag the same concept, the strings don't match:

Concept	Web channel	App attribute_channel
Account/Profile	Account	Me
Shop	Shop	Shop
Home	Home	Home
Benefits	Benefits	Benefits
Rewards	Tuesdays	Tuesdays
If you want apples-to-apples cross-channel reporting (e.g., "total Account traffic"), you'll need a normalization CASE in addition to the COALESCE — something like:

CASE
  WHEN unified_channel IN ('Account', 'Me', 'Mi Cuenta') THEN 'MyAccount'
  WHEN unified_channel IN ('Shop', 'Compra')             THEN 'Shop'
  ...
END AS normalized_channel

```
END AS unified_channel
```

Session tables (session_summary_fact, session_event_fact) do not carry channel columns — you must get channel from UDI.

📍 REGION / GEOGRAPHY INFO
There is no single column for region. You have to compose it from multiple signals and join to a ZIP dimension. Here's the realistic picture.

Table 1 — unified_digital_interactions (hit level)
Column	Scope	Sep 24 Fill	Granularity
geo_postal_code	Web	~46% of web hits	ZIP → join to state/region
attribute_country	App	~99% of app hits	Country only (US/intl)
coverage_map_location	Both	<0.1%	City, state string (coverage check only)
Table 2 — session_summary_fact (session level)
No geo columns. Must derive region from UDI and roll up to session via session_id.

Table 3 — session_event_fact (session event level)
No geo columns. Join bridge between SSF and UDI via row_identity_hash + event_date + source_table.

Table 4 — ZIP dimension (for Web region lookup)
`BDM_MKTGANALYTICS_DB.FEDERATED_T.MKTGANALYTICS_V_GEO_DIM_ZIP` (41,829 rows, refreshed daily)

Keyed by ZIP_CODE, provides STATE, REGION, MARKET fields. Let me confirm the column names.My role can't access that database directly, but it exists and your role should. Let me also check if there are address tables for the authenticated-user geography path.---

🎯 THE THREE PATHS TO GET REGION
🥇 Path 1: Web — ZIP → State/Region (Best coverage: ~46% of web hits)
Tables: UDI + ZIP dimension

SELECT
  u.row_identity_hash,
  u.event_date,
  u.geo_postal_code,
  z.STATE,
  z.REGION,
  z.MARKET
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions u
LEFT JOIN BDM_MKTGANALYTICS_DB.FEDERATED_T.MKTGANALYTICS_V_GEO_DIM_ZIP z
  ON LEFT(u.geo_postal_code, 5) = z.ZIP_CODE
WHERE u.source_table = 't_web_interactions'
  AND u.event_date = DATE '2026-09-24'
  AND u.geo_postal_code IS NOT NULL
Coverage: 46% of web hits → state/region.
Limitation: Web only. Apps return 0% because geo_postal_code is never populated on app.

🥈 Path 2: App — Country Only (Best coverage: ~99% of app hits)
Table: UDI only

SELECT
  row_identity_hash,
  event_date,
  attribute_country
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions
WHERE source_table = 't_app_interactions'
  AND event_date = DATE '2026-09-24'
Coverage: ~99% of app hits → country.
Limitation: Country only. No state, no region, no city.

🥉 Path 3: Authenticated Customer Address Join (Both channels, ~50% of auth'd traffic)
For authenticated users (user_auth_state = 'Authenticated'), you can join to the account billing address.

Candidate address tables (confirm access with your role):
- BDM_MKTGANALYTICS_DB.FEDERATED_T.MKTGANALYTICS_V_GEO_DIM_ZIP
- BDM_MKTGANALYTICS_DB.POSTPAID_V.LOCATION_ADDRESS
- BDM_MKTGANALYTICS_DB.MKTGANALYTICS_V.LOCATION_ACCOUNT_ADDRESS

SELECT
  u.row_identity_hash,
  u.customer_id,
  u.encrypted_ban,
  addr.state,
  addr.region,
  addr.market
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions u
JOIN <customer_address_table> addr
  ON u.encrypted_ban = addr.encrypted_ban  -- or canonical_user_id, confirm key with Marketing Analytics team
WHERE u.user_auth_state = 'Authenticated'
  AND u.event_date = DATE '2026-09-24'
Coverage: Works for both app and web, but only for the ~50% of traffic that is authenticated. Fills the app region gap for logged-in customers.
Limitation: Won't help prospects or logged-out users.

📊 Realistic Combined Region Coverage (if you stack all three)
Scope	Source	Coverage
Web hits with state/region	ZIP → GEO_DIM_ZIP	~46%
Web hits with country	Infer US from ZIP presence	~46%
App hits with country	attribute_country	~99%
App hits with state/region	Customer address (auth only)	~30% (half of ~60% auth app traffic)
Any hit with country	Combined	~85%
Any hit with state/region	Combined	~40%
📝 Recommended Bronze projection for region
```
-- Add to your Bronze SELECT:
geo_postal_code, -- raw web ZIP
attribute_country, -- raw app country

-- Then in a downstream Silver enrichment layer:
LEFT(geo_postal_code, 5) AS zip5, -- normalized ZIP for join
COALESCE(z.STATE, auth_addr.state) AS state, -- web ZIP → state, else auth address
COALESCE(z.REGION, auth_addr.region) AS region,
COALESCE(z.MARKET, auth_addr.market) AS market,
CASE
WHEN geo_postal_code IS NOT NULL THEN 'US' -- ZIP implies US
ELSE attribute_country
END AS country
```

For your immediate MIP Bronze projection: keep what you already have (geo_postal_code + attribute_country). The region enrichment belongs in a Silver layer step with the ZIP + address joins, not Bronze.

🎯 Final answer in one sentence
Channel info: unified_digital_interactions.channel (web) + unified_digital_interactions.attribute_channel (app).

Region info: unified_digital_interactions.geo_postal_code (web, 46%) joined to BDM_MKTGANALYTICS_DB.FEDERATED_T.MKTGANALYTICS_V_GEO_DIM_ZIP for state/region, plus unified_digital_interactions.attribute_country (app, 99%) for country, plus a customer-address join for authenticated users to close the app state/region gap.

Unified channel pattern
```
CASE
WHEN source_table = 't_web_interactions' THEN channel
WHEN source_table = 't_app_interactions' THEN attribute_channel
END AS unified_channel
```

Session tables (session_summary_fact, session_event_fact) do not carry channel columns — you must get channel from UDI.

📍 REGION / GEOGRAPHY INFO
There is no single column for region. You have to compose it from multiple signals and join to a ZIP dimension. Here's the realistic picture.

Table 1 — unified_digital_interactions (hit level)
Column	Scope	Sep 24 Fill	Granularity
geo_postal_code	Web	~46% of web hits	ZIP → join to state/region
attribute_country	App	~99% of app hits	Country only (US/intl)
coverage_map_location	Both	<0.1%	City, state string (coverage check only)
Table 2 — session_summary_fact (session level)
No geo columns. Must derive region from UDI and roll up to session via session_id.

Table 3 — session_event_fact (session event level)
No geo columns. Join bridge between SSF and UDI via row_identity_hash + event_date + source_table.

Table 4 — ZIP dimension (for Web region lookup)
`BDM_MKTGANALYTICS_DB.FEDERATED_T.MKTGANALYTICS_V_GEO_DIM_ZIP` (41,829 rows, refreshed daily)

Keyed by ZIP_CODE, provides STATE, REGION, MARKET fields. Let me confirm the column names.My role can't access that database directly, but it exists and your role should. Let me also check if there are address tables for the authenticated-user geography path.---

🎯 THE THREE PATHS TO GET REGION
🥇 Path 1: Web — ZIP → State/Region (Best coverage: ~46% of web hits)
Tables: UDI + ZIP dimension

  u.row_identity_hash,
  u.event_date,
  u.geo_postal_code,

LEFT JOIN BDM_MKTGANALYTICS_DB.FEDERATED_T.MKTGANALYTICS_V_GEO_DIM_ZIP z
  ON LEFT(u.geo_postal_code, 5) = z.ZIP_CODE
WHERE u.source_table = 't_web_interactions'
  AND u.event_date = DATE '2026-09-24'
  AND u.geo_postal_code IS NOT NULL

so there is no paid search?
02:26 AM
Eddy
Correct — No "Paid Search" Channel Value
Neither channel (web) nor attribute_channel (app) carries a marketing acquisition channel like "Paid Search", "Organic Search", "Direct", "Social", "Email", etc.

🎯 Why — these columns mean something different
Column	What it actually represents
channel / attribute_channel	Site section / app tab the user is in — "Shop", "My Account", "HSI". This is a content/navigation dimension.
"Paid Search" is a traffic source / acquisition dimension — how the user arrived, not where they are on the site. That's a different concept entirely.

🔍 Where acquisition channel actually lives
Let me verify by checking for any marketing-channel column in the schema.Confirmed — nothing in Silver UDI or the session tables uses the word "channel" to mean marketing/acquisition. The channel columns are purely site-section.

🧭 How to derive acquisition channel yourself
You can build "Paid Search / Organic / Direct / Email / Social" from signals you already have:

Signals available
Signal	Column	Source Table	Fill
UTM parameters (source/medium)	page_url_full / entry_page_url_full	UDI / SSF	Web only, ~10% of sessions
Adobe campaign code	external_campaign_code	UDI	Web ~6%, App 0.02%
Referring domain	entry_page_referrer	SSF (access-controlled)	Web only
Derivation pattern (session-level, web only)
SELECT
  session_id,
  entry_page_url_full,
  entry_page_referrer,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_medium=([^&#]+)', 1) AS utm_medium,
  REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_source=([^&#]+)', 1) AS utm_source,
  CASE
    -- Paid search
    WHEN LOWER(REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_medium=([^&#]+)', 1))
           IN ('cpc','ppc','paid','paidsearch','sem')
      THEN 'Paid Search'
    -- Paid social
    WHEN LOWER(REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_medium=([^&#]+)', 1))
           IN ('paidsocial','social-paid','social_cpc')
      THEN 'Paid Social'
    -- Email
    WHEN LOWER(REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_source=([^&#]+)', 1)) = 'email'
      OR LOWER(REGEXP_EXTRACT(entry_page_url_full, '[?&]utm_medium=([^&#]+)', 1)) LIKE '%email%'
      THEN 'Email'
    -- Organic search
    WHEN entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%google.com%'
      OR entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%bing.com%'
      OR entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%yahoo.com%'
      OR entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%duckduckgo.com%'
      THEN 'Organic Search'
    -- Organic social
    WHEN entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%facebook.com%'
      OR entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%instagram.com%'
      OR entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%twitter.com%'
      OR entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%t.co%'
      OR entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%linkedin.com%'
      OR entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%youtube.com%'
      OR entry_page_referrer ILIKE '%tiktok.com%'
      THEN 'Organic Social'
    -- Referral
    WHEN entry_page_referrer IS NOT NULL AND entry_page_referrer <> ''
      THEN 'Referral'
    -- Direct
    ELSE 'Direct / Unknown'
  END AS acquisition_channel
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_summary_fact
WHERE source_table = 't_web_interactions'
  AND session_status IN ('OPEN','CLOSED')
  AND session_start_date = DATE '2026-09-24'
Known gaps
Gap	Reason
No acquisition channel for App	App has no entry_page_referrer and ~0% UTM. Need Adjust/Branch/AppsFlyer attribution data.
~90% of web sessions have no UTM	Only ~10% of sessions are UTM-tagged. The rest need to lean on entry_page_referrer to classify.
`entry_page_referrer` is access-controlled	Confirm your role has r_cdw_snf_dbi_prd_interaction_analyst with URL access group, or it returns REDACTED.
📝 Bottom line
"Paid Search" as a Silver column: does not exist.
To get it for Web: derive from UTM (utm_medium = 'cpc') + entry_page_referrer on session_summary_fact. Reaches maybe 40–60% of web sessions depending on how aggressive your classification is.
To get it for App: not possible with UDI/SEF/SSF — need marketing attribution partner data.
If a durable acquisition_channel column matters for your consumers, that's a strong candidate for a Silver enhancement ticket under DIRT-691 — the parsing logic should run once at ingestion, not every time someone queries.

Want me to run the classification above against Sep 24 to show you the acquisition-channel distribution?

🧭 Key observations
99% US-dominant — this is a US-focused app, as expected.
~1% non-US / unknown combined — includes real international travelers (US customers abroad on roaming, or international prospects) plus data-quality nulls.
Standard ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 codes — two-letter country codes, uppercase. Join-ready against any standard country dimension.
`??` (42K rows) is a known OneMind sentinel for "country lookup failed" — treat it as unknown.
Empty string vs NULL both mean "no country detected." Normalize them together: NULLIF(TRIM(attribute_country), '') IS NULL.
🔧 Clean normalization pattern
CASE
  WHEN NULLIF(TRIM(attribute_country), '') IS NULL THEN 'UNKNOWN'
  WHEN attribute_country = '??'                     THEN 'UNKNOWN'
  ELSE UPPER(attribute_country)
END AS country_code
💡 Practical implication
For region derivation on app, attribute_country = 'US' is essentially the whole dataset — you can safely assume US geography for app region work. The ~2.2M non-US/unknown rows are a rounding error for most reporting but important to filter out of US-only KPIs (e.g., "US customers active in app").

🧭 What each value means
Value	What it is

🔀 Pipe-concatenated values (A\|B)
When site_name has a | in it, the hit was tagged with both site identifiers — common when:
- A user flow spans properties (e.g., prospect lands on TLife App and gets routed through Metro App)
- An embedded web view inside one app renders content from another property
- TLife App|TLife App is a tagging artifact where the same value gets emitted twice

💡 So for your "METRO" and "RETAIL" question
Metro App: site_name = 'Metro App' OR site_name LIKE '%Metro App%' → ~32M hits/day combined (3.9% standalone + 7.5% in combo = ~11% of all app hits touch Metro)
TRetail App: site_name = 'TRetail App' OR site_name LIKE '%TRetail App%' → ~18M hits/day combined (~6% of all hits involve the retail employee app)
Metro web: site_name = 'MbyT' → ~1M hits/day
🔧 Clean property breakdown
CASE
  WHEN site_name ILIKE '%Metro App%'    THEN 'Metro App'
  WHEN site_name ILIKE '%TRetail App%'  THEN 'Retail App'
  WHEN site_name ILIKE '%TLife App%'    THEN 'T-Life App'
  WHEN site_name ILIKE '%MbyT%'         THEN 'Metro Web'
  WHEN site_name ILIKE '%T-Mo Prepaid%' THEN 'Prepaid Web'
  WHEN site_name ILIKE '%TFB%'          THEN 'TFB Web'
  WHEN site_name = 'TMO'                THEN 'T-Mobile Web'
  WHEN site_name ILIKE '%Expert Assist%' THEN 'Expert Assist'
  WHEN site_name ILIKE '%TMO Concierge%' THEN 'Concierge'
  ELSE 'Other'
END AS property
🪤 Important for your Bronze projection
Your current MIP pipeline uses source_table + page_app_type to distinguish properties, but site_name is the finer-grained signal — especially for separating TLife vs Metro vs TRetail, which all share source_table = 't_app_interactions'.

If downstream consumers need to split Metro from TLife or exclude retail-employee traffic, add site_name to your projection (it's already in your SELECT list — good) and build the property CASE above in a Silver enrichment step.


SELECT
    event_date,
    site_name,
    attribute_country,
    COUNT(*) AS rows
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions
WHERE source_table = 't_app_interactions'
  AND event_date IN (DATE '2026-09-24', DATE '2026-09-29')
  AND (
        attribute_country IS NULL
        OR TRIM(attribute_country) = ''
        OR attribute_country IN ('METRO','RETAIL','??')
        OR LENGTH(TRIM(attribute_country)) <> 2
      )
GROUP BY event_date, site_name, attribute_country
ORDER BY event_date, rows DESC;

SELECT
    MAX(timestampdiff(MINUTE,session_start_time,session_end_time)) AS maxSessionMinutes,
    percentile_approx(
        timestampdiff(MINUTE,session_start_time,session_end_time),
        array(0.50,0.90,0.95,0.99,0.999)
    ) AS sessionDurationPercentiles
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_summary_fact
WHERE session_start_date = DATE '2026-09-24'
  AND source_table='t_web_interactions'
  AND session_status IN ('OPEN','CLOSED');
output:
maxSessionMinutes	sessionDurationPercentiles
6846	[0,10,21,49,142]

WITH scoped AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT
        session_id,
        event_date
    FROM prdrzranalytics.lab42.sdi_tbl_mip_bronze_edlSessionEventFact_daily
    WHERE event_date BETWEEN DATE '2026-09-24' AND DATE '2026-10-03'
)
SELECT
    datediff(
        s.event_date,
        ssf.session_start_date_pst
    ) AS daysAfterSessionStart,
    COUNT(*) AS sessionDatePairs
FROM scoped s
JOIN prdrzranalytics.lab42.sdi_tbl_mip_bronze_edlSessionSummaryFact_daily ssf
  ON ssf.session_id=s.session_id
WHERE ssf.session_status IN ('OPEN','CLOSED')
GROUP BY
    datediff(s.event_date,ssf.session_start_date_pst)
ORDER BY daysAfterSessionStart DESC;
output:
daysAfterSessionStart	sessionDatePairs
15	1

8	2

6	3

4	13
3	28
2	101
1	16561335
0	56408932

================================================================================================
DECISION GUIDE

1. DAILY_SEQUENTIAL faster than WINDOW:
   Daily chunking reduces the working set even without concurrency.

2. DAILY_PARALLEL materially faster with failedCalls=0:
   Daily chunking + concurrency is promising. Re-test that pattern in Lakeflow
   before adopting it for production.

3. DAILY_PARALLEL reports concurrent-write/Delta conflicts:
   Keep concurrency low or use sequential daily chunks.

4. WINDOW remains fastest:
   Keep multi-day window execution for B01; concurrency is not helping here.

For the default two-day test, concurrency above 2 cannot be evaluated.

I am a bit skeptical on the joins we have. UDI has an average of 300MM rows per day, sessions fact also have average of 300MM rows per day, whereas sessions summary only has 300MM in total. I ran our query just for 3 days, but the join shows we read more than 15B rows. I wonder if we are missing a join criteria. The initial query ran more than 2 hours and hasn't completed so I just cancelled it.


WITH base AS (
    SELECT
        u.event_date,
        ss.session_start_date,
        u.site_name,
        u.channel_name,
        ss.canonical_user_id,
        u.event_page_view,
        u.event_purchase,
        u.event_click_to_call,
        u.event_chat_engage
    FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions u
    INNER JOIN prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_event_fact sef
        ON u.row_identity_hash = sef.row_identity_hash
       AND u.event_date = sef.event_date
       AND u.source_table = sef.source_table
    INNER JOIN prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_summary_fact ss
        ON sef.session_id = ss.session_id
    WHERE u.event_date BETWEEN DATE '2026-09-20' AND DATE '2026-10-03'
)
 
SELECT
    session_start_date,
    site_name,
    coalesce(channel_name,'(not set)') AS channel_name,
 
    COUNT(*) AS hits,
    COUNT(DISTINCT canonical_user_id) AS visitors,
    SUM(coalesce(event_page_view,0)) AS page_views,
    SUM(coalesce(event_purchase,0)) AS orders,
    SUM(coalesce(event_click_to_call,0)) AS vr_calls,
    SUM(coalesce(event_chat_engage,0)) AS vr_chats
 
FROM base
GROUP BY
    session_start_date,
    site_name,
    coalesce(channel_name,'(not set)')
ORDER BY hits DESC;

SELECT
    session_id,
    COUNT(*) AS rows,
    COUNT(DISTINCT source_table) AS sourceTables
FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_summary_fact
GROUP BY session_id
HAVING COUNT(*) > 1
LIMIT 100;



The scan concern is valid, but our cardinality tests show the join itself is not multiplying rows. UDI and SEF matched 1:1 on `row_identity_hash + event_date + source_table`, and UDI → SEF → SSF also stayed one row per hit.

In Silver 01, we already scope SEF to the requested `event_date` window first, join it to SSF by `session_id`, then join to UDI using the composite key while also filtering UDI to the same date window. So I think the next step is to test the raw tables the same way with a true 1–3 day range before changing the join.

```sql
WITH udi AS (
    SELECT
        row_identity_hash,
        event_date,
        source_table,
        site_name,
        channel_name,
        event_page_view,
        event_purchase,
        event_click_to_call,
        event_chat_engage
    FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.unified_digital_interactions
    WHERE event_date BETWEEN DATE '2026-09-20' AND DATE '2026-09-22'
),
sef AS (
    SELECT
        row_identity_hash,
        event_date,
        source_table,
        session_id
    FROM prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_event_fact
    WHERE event_date BETWEEN DATE '2026-09-20' AND DATE '2026-09-22'
),
base AS (
    SELECT
        u.event_date,
        ss.session_start_date,
        u.site_name,
        u.channel_name,
        ss.canonical_user_id,
        u.event_page_view,
        u.event_purchase,
        u.event_click_to_call,
        u.event_chat_engage
    FROM udi u
    INNER JOIN sef
        ON u.row_identity_hash = sef.row_identity_hash
       AND u.event_date = sef.event_date
       AND u.source_table = sef.source_table
    INNER JOIN prd_dbi_analytics.silver_digital_interactions.session_summary_fact ss
        ON sef.session_id = ss.session_id
)
SELECT
    session_start_date,
    site_name,
    coalesce(channel_name,'(not set)') AS channel_name,
    COUNT(*) AS hits,
    COUNT(DISTINCT canonical_user_id) AS visitors,
    SUM(coalesce(event_page_view,0)) AS page_views,
    SUM(coalesce(event_purchase,0)) AS orders,
    SUM(coalesce(event_click_to_call,0)) AS vr_calls,
    SUM(coalesce(event_chat_engage,0)) AS vr_chats
FROM base
GROUP BY
    session_start_date,
    site_name,
    coalesce(channel_name,'(not set)')
ORDER BY hits DESC;
```

[DELTA_MULTIPLE_SOURCE_ROW_MATCHING_TARGET_ROW_IN_MERGE] Cannot perform Merge as multiple source rows matched and attempted to modify the same
target row in the Delta table in possibly conflicting ways. By SQL semantics of Merge,
when multiple source rows match on the same target row, the result may be ambiguous
as it is unclear which source row should be used to update or delete the matching
target row. You can preprocess the source table to eliminate the possibility of
multiple matches. Please refer to
https://docs.microsoft.com/azure/databricks/delta/merge#merge-error
